1. From H to O:
   - H → I → F → B → O
   - H → I → F → B → E → L → M → J → P → O
   - H → I → F → B → N → Y → P → O

   - H → X → G → C → Y → N → B → O
   - H → X → K → C → Y → P → O
   - H → X → K → C → Y → N → B → O

Now, we need to find the minimum number of edges to remove to disconnect H and O. Notice that removing the edge H → X would disconnect H from the rest of the graph, which includes O. However, we need to check if there's a more efficient way.

By examining the paths, we see that removing the edges H → X and H → I would disconnect H from all paths leading to O. Therefore, the minimum number of edges to remove is 2.

Image name: PGS_task2_gt3_PAIR2
Task: task2_min_nodes_removal
Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b
Mode: zero_shot, COT: cot, Attempt: 16
--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to disconnect the two black nodes labeled "E" and "H", we need to identify all paths connecting these two nodes and then find the critical nodes (bottlenecks) whose removal will break all paths between them.

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify the two black nodes:** "E" and "H".
2. **Find all immediate neighbors of "E" and "H":**
   - "E" is connected to: L, B, and F.
   - "H" is connected to: B, G, and C.

3. **Trace paths from "E" to "H":**
   - Path 1: E -> B -> H
   - Path 2: E -> B -> N -> C -> H
   - Path 3: E -> L -> M -> F -> X -> G -> H
   - Path 4: E -> F -> X -> G -> H
   - Path 5: E -> F -> K -> J -> I -> C -> H
   - Path 6: E -> F -> K -> J -> P -> C -> H

4. **Evaluate bottleneck nodes:**
   - Node "B" appears in Path 1 and Path 2.
   - Node "C" appears in Path 2, Path 5, and Path 6.
   - Node "F" appears in Path 3 and Path 4.
   - Node "G" appears in Path 3 and Path 4.

   Removing "B" and "C" doesnt remove the path through F and G.

5. **Find the minimum set of nodes to remove:**
   - Removing "B" and "F" will break Path 1, Path 2, and Path 4 but not reach "H".
   - Therefore, we need to remove "B" and "F" and "I".

Thus, the minimum number of nodes that need to be removed is 3: B, F, and I.

```
Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [B, F, I]
```
--- Ground Truth ---

Image name: SEIS_task3_gt2_PAIR4
Task: task3_min_edges_removal
Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b
Mode: zero_shot, COT: cot, Attempt: 16
--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes \( L \) and \( J \), we need to identify all paths between these two nodes and find the minimal cut set that disconnects them.

1. **Identify the black nodes and their immediate neighbors:**
   - Node \( L \) is connected to node \( E \).
   - Node \( J \) is connected to node \( P \).

2. **Trace the paths from \( L \) to \( E \) and then to other nodes:**
   - \( L \) -> \( E \)
   - From \( E \), the paths continue to \( G \), \( H \), \( I \), \( K \), \( X \), \( M \).

3. **Trace the paths from \( J \) to \( P \) and then to other nodes:**
   - \( J \) -> \( P \)
   - From \( P \), the paths continue to \( I \), \( H \), \( G \), \( C \), \( O \), \( D \).

4. **Identify the overlapping nodes and edges:**
   - \( E \) connects to \( G \), \( H \), \( X \), \( F \), \( M \).
   - \( G \) connects to \( H \), \( C \), \( M \).
   - \( H \) connects to \( I \), \( C \), \( G \).
   - \( I \) connects to \( P \), \( H \), \( O \), \( C \).
   - \( P \) connects to \( J \), \( I \), \( O \).

5. **Find the minimal cut set to disconnect \( L \) and \( J \):**
   - Removing \( E \) -> \( G \) disconnects one path.
   - Removing \( H \) -> \( I \) disconnects another path.
   - Removing \( G \) -> \( H \) ensures a complete disconnect.

Thus, the minimal cut set is \( (E, G) \), \( (H, I) \), and \( (G, H) \).

However, upon reviewing, we can see that removing \( (E, G) \) and \( (H, I) \) might actually suffice because these remove the critical links in the overlapping paths.

**Final Answer:**

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(E, G), (H, I)].
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 2, 'cut_set': [['E', 'L'], ['L', 'M']]}

3. **Trace the paths between N and P:**
   - Path 1: N - B - H - G - K - F - E - M - J - P
   - Path 2: N - Y - O - C - I - J - P
   - Path 3: N - B - H - G - K - F - E - L - C - I - J - P

4. **Find the bottlenecks or critical nodes that, if removed, will disconnect N and P:**
   - Removing node B disconnects the path through B.
   - Removing node J disconnects the path through J.
   - Removing node F disconnects the path through F.

By removing nodes B, J, and F, all paths between N and P are disconnected.

1. **Examine the two black nodes \( H \) and \( N \).**

2. **Identify the immediate neighboring nodes of the black nodes:**
   - Node \( H \) is connected to nodes \( G \), \( B \), and \( I \).
   - Node \( N \) is connected to nodes \( C \) and \( O \).

3. **Iteratively find other neighboring nodes:**
   - For \( H \):
     - \( G \) is connected to \( X \) and \( K \).
     - \( B \) is connected to \( E \) and \( C \).
     - \( I \) is connected to \( P \) and \( J \).
   - For \( N \):
     - \( C \) is connected to \( B \) and \( O \).
     - \( O \) is connected to \( P \) and \( Y \).

4. **Evaluate the found paths:**
   - Path from \( H \) to \( N \) through \( B \) and \( C \): \( H \rightarrow B \rightarrow C \rightarrow N \).
   - Path from \( H \) to \( N \) through \( B \), \( E \), \( M \), and \( L \): \( H \rightarrow B \rightarrow E \rightarrow M \rightarrow L \).
   - Path from \( H \) to \( N \) through \( B \), \( E \), and \( F \): \( H \rightarrow B \rightarrow E \rightarrow F \).
   - Path from \( H \) to \( N \) through \( I \), \( P \), and \( O \): \( H \rightarrow I \rightarrow P \rightarrow O \rightarrow N \).

5. **Identify the minimum cut set:**
   - Removing edge \( (B, C) \) cuts the path \( H \rightarrow B \rightarrow C \rightarrow N \).
   - Removing edge \( (B, E) \) cuts the paths \( H \rightarrow B \rightarrow E \rightarrow M \rightarrow L \) and \( H \rightarrow B \rightarrow E \rightarrow F \).
   - Removing edge \( (P, O) \) cuts the path \( H \rightarrow I \rightarrow P \rightarrow O \rightarrow N \).

Thus, the minimum number of edges to remove is 3, and the cut set is \( [(B, C), (B, E), (P, O)] \).
